 2. A.   04.14.15 
2. RECOGNITION 
A. TSD Mission Moment – Smith Student Fundraising Initiative 
Calen Knight is a sixth grade student at Smith Middle School. She is being 
honored tonight for her efforts to create a charity to  help make her 
community a better place. When Calen was seven, she decided to donate 
some of her stuffed animals and toys to children at Lighthouse of Oakland 
County. She wanted to help kids her age who didn’t have the things she 
did. Then she learned that chil dren needed more—so she started to gather 
clothing, winter coats, anything she discovered there was a need for. 
Eventually, Calen started her own charity, Calen4kids.org and began 
recruiting her classmates and friends to help. 
Tonight, we recognize Calen in our Mission Moment as a leader in 
community outreach and charity for the entire region. Calen, would you 
please come up and tell us a little more about your efforts to help children 
and families through Calen4kids.org? 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Calen Knight will give a short presentation on 
her charity and receive a certificate from the Board. 
3
2. B. 04.14.15 
2. RECOGNITION 
B.  Oakland County Outstanding Teacher of the Year Awards 
The Troy School District has many outstanding teachers on its staff. This 
evening we are proud to recognize the accomplishments of sixteen 
individuals who were nominated for the Troy School District Outstanding 
Teacher Award Program. 
The nominees represent exemplary educators at every level and were 
nominated by their peers, by parents and by former and current 
students, all of whom talked about the impact these teachers have on 
the lives of those around them. Their nomination letters described how 
the professionals we honor here tonight go above and beyond to 
ensure that their students learn, grow and thrive.  
Would you please come forward as your name is called? (Di stribute 
certificates/plants.) 
First, we honor thirteen outstanding nominees, who have touched the 
lives of countless students and families in the Troy School District. We 
are grateful for their dedication and passion for student success. 
Erica Bachman, 3rd Grade, Wattles Elementary School 
Nadeen Brown, First Grade, Leonard Elementary School 
Adam Burns, English, Innovations, TV Production, Athens High School 
Karen Bush, 2nd Grade, Schroeder Elementary 
Jason Cichowicz, 5th Grade, Schroeder Elementary School 
Jennifer DeWard, 4th Grade, Leonard Elementary School 
Lora Herbert, 5th Grade, Hill Elementary School 
Sara Jozefiak, 2nd Grade, Barnard Elementary School 
Alan MacNair, Orchestra, Troy High School 
Grayson McKinney, 4th Grade, Costello Elementary School 
Debbie Motherwell, 2nd Grade, Martell Elementary School 
Kristy Pierce, Economics, Government, Leadership, Athens High School 
Amy Wallace, 4th Grade, Leonard Elementary School 
Three of our district nominees were sent to Oakland Schools to 
represent Troy in the county-wide outstanding teacher award program, 
administered by Oakland Schools. 
4
Katie Held, Kindergarten Teacher at Troy Union Elementary was nominated at 
the elementary level; Charlie Tull, Math a n d  T e c h n o l o g y  Teacher from 
Larson Middle School was nominated at the middle school level; and Jane Moss, 
Chemistry Teacher from A t h e n s High School was nominated at the high

Year and was awarded a  free one-year lease of a car, donated through the

TEA selection committee to represent the District.

                 2. C.    04.14.15 
2.       RECOGNITION 
C. Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence Mini-Grant Recipients 
This evening we are pleased to recognize the Troy Foundation for 
Educational Excellence and the work they do to support our schools, our 
students and our staff. The Foundation awards grants to help fund projects 
that support the curriculum in every school in the Troy S chool District. This 
year, the Foundation was able to fund  81 grants for a diverse group of 
projects to help enhance learning for students in every school in the 
District. That kind of support is invaluable. 
The Foundation is made up of volunteers who give of their time and talents 
to raise money and support innovative projects that enrich student 
learning. Again, these projects take place in every school in our District and 
many would not be possible without the resources the Foundation 
provides. 
At this time, I would like to introduce  Al Robinson, President of the Troy 
Foundation for Educational Excellence, who will briefly  recognize the most 
recent group of grant recipients and the many diverse ways they are 
enhancing the education of our Troy School District students. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Names of recipients/projects on screen as Mr. Robinson 
speaks.

TSD Mission Moment 
honored tonight for their successful implementation and 
development of a mission statement for the school. The 
Leader in Me is based on the “7 Habits of Highly Effective 
People,”  by  Stephen  Covey.  Students  are  taught 
leadership  principles  and  strategies  based  on  the  7 
Habits. During the summer of 2014, teachers conducted a 
book study on this program to determine its viability for 
our  community.  Team  members  spent  countless  hours 
writing  multiple  grants  securing  over  $50,000  to 
implement  Leader  in  Me.  Over  five  days  of  training  has 
taken place during the summer and weekends to prepare 
teachers, support staff, parents, and PTO board members 
for the implementation of this initiative. 
Research indicates the Leader in Me program has positive 
effects  in  numerous  areas  including  a  student’s  self‐
confidence,  responsibility,  leadership,  problem  solving, 
and  communication  as  well  as  cultivating  a  positive 
culture.  At Martell, the  entire  community  has embraced 
this initiative and results are already being seen. The Troy 
Board  of  Education  is  excited  about  this  opportunity  to 
develop  and  sharpen  students’  abilities  and  tonight  we 
are  pleased  to  recognize  the  following  people,  after 
which Mr. Lesser will tell us a little more about the work 
that has taken place.

Students  and  staff  at  Martell  Elementary  are  being 
integration  of  the
  Leader  in  Me  Program  and  their 
Month,  in  recognition  of  the  outstanding  work  of 
building‐level  educational  leaders.  This  evening,  the 
Board will pass a resolution to commend elementary and 
secondary principals for their exceptional commitment to 
excellence in the Troy School District.  
It was moved by Ms. Underwood and supported by Mrs. 
Fleming that  
WHEREAS, energetic  and  inspiring  school  leadership  is 
essential  if  teachers,  schools  and  districts  are  to  ensure 
that all Troy students are college‐ and career‐ready; and, 
WHEREAS, K‐12  school  principals  play  a  vital  role  in  the 
success  of  students  and  act  as  the  liaison  between  the 
school  and  the  community  it  serves,  ensuring  that 
parents  and  the  community  are  aware  of  student  and 
school achievements; and, 
WHEREAS, Troy  School  District  principals  are 
dedicated, quality  educational  professionals  committed 
to providing sound leadership and guidance to their staff 
and students; and,  
WHEREAS, during  this  month,  we  join  with  educators, 
parents  and  students  throughout  Michigan  to  raise 
awareness  of  the  importance  of  educational  leadership, 
and to recognize and thank all principals working in Troy 
for their exemplary service; 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that the Troy Board of 
Education  officially  recognizes  February  as  Principal 
Appreciation  Month  in  Troy  and  thanks  our  dedicated 
building leaders for all they do for our students and staff.

Troy Board of Education Meeting 4 March 03, 2015  
13
Principal Appreciation Month 
15‐025 
Throughout  the  State  of  Michigan,  February  has 
traditionally  been  celebrated  as  Principal  Appreciation 
Spirit of Diversity Award 
which  were  designed  by  the  Troy  Diversity  &  Inclusion 
Council  as  a  way  to  honor  those  who  go  above  and 
diversity is celebrated within the Troy School District.   At 
this  evening’s  meeting,  the  Board  recognized  eight 
individuals  who  have  helped  to  foster  a  climate  of 
inclusion  and  diversity  creating  an  optimal  learning 
environment  for  the  entire  school  community.   These 
awards  will  be  continued  for  years  to  come  as  a  way  to 
recognize the wonderful work that is done in our school 
community.  The  following  individuals  were  recognized 
and asked to step forward to receive a certificate:   Nancy 
Lining – teacher at Larson Middle School, Dawn Hayes – 
teacher  at  Athens  High  School,  Jen  Hilzinger  –  parent 
volunteer,  Kerry  Brennan  –  teacher  at  Wattles 
Elementary,  Lori  Medina‐Yashinski  –  parent  volunteer, 
Lisa  Carruthers  –  teacher  at  Leonard  Elementary, 
Stephanie Sacco – parent volunteer, and Nadeen Brown – 
teacher at Leonard Elementary.

CURRICULUM 
Schools Closed to Open 
Education adopts a resolution to close Bemis Elementary 
and Schroeder Elementary School as well as Athens High 
and  Troy  High  School  to  open  enrollment  for  the 
2015/2016  school  year,  with  the  exception  of  not  more 
than 25 incoming ninth grade students who reside in the 
Troy  High  School  attendance  area,  but  wish  to  attend 
Athens  High  School.   Students  with  siblings  attending 
Athens  High  School  and  currently  enrolled  in  grades  9  – 
11  will  be  allowed  to  open  enroll  to  Athens  for  the 
2015/2016 school year.

Oakland County Special 
special education programming at the county level.   She 
first  reviewed  in‐District  data  related  to  our  current 
students,  staffing,  programs  and  services.    She 
mentioned  the  factors  that  have  affected  services  and 
supports for students with disabilities in Troy.   She then 
discussed  the  changes  taking  place  at  the  county  level, 
which  include  demographic  changes,  state  performance 
plan  targets,  instructional  changes  in  general  education, 
and  changes  in  achievement  expectations  and 
accountability.   These  changes  are  the  result  of  the 
Oakland  County  Center  Program  Redesign.    She  shared 
that  recommendations  would  be  made  in  the  spring  of 
2015  to  ensure  continuous  improvement  of  positive 
outcomes  for  all  students  with  disabilities  in  the  Troy 
School District.   
There was some discussion by Board members regarding 
their  detailed  review  of  this  information,  which  was 
presented at the February workshop.

 B. REPORT:   Reading Recovery TLL Pilot Update 
 The District initiated a Pilot of Reading Recovery, Kinder-Core, and Teaching 
and Learning Leaders at the beginning of the 2013-2014 school year.  The 
purpose of the pilot is to address the achievement gap by providing early 
intervention and expert instruction to struggling readers.  Mrs. Haezebrouck 
will provide a brief update on how the pilot is progressing.  
  RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This a report – no action is necessary.

learning.

What is Biotechnology? 
Biotechnology is the use of 
biological processes, organisms, 
or systems to manufacture 
products intended to improve the 
quality of human life.

154
Are you a ‘taster’? 
• What accounts for the reason why people  desire 
certain foods that others don’t care for? 
• Genes! 
• Any person with a single functional copy of the 
PTC gene can make the protein and is sensitive to 
PTC.  Some taste a stronger bitterness than 
others. 
• What causes the variation of bitterness? 
• Why can offspring have different sensitivity to the 
taste?

an experimental method to test it.  
electrophoresis and genetic principles.  
from given phenotype and pedigree 
information.

How has the equipment improved 
students’ understanding of genetics? 
• Students made connections with content 
throughout the genetics unit. 
• More opportunities for meaning-making 
• Engaged, rich dialogue about the process of 
gel electrophoresis and why DNA fragments 
result in different sizes due to restriction 
enzymes cut. 
• Students determined the method of 
inheritance for PTC.

• Impart technology literacy 
• Allow students to excel in a technologically 
advanced society in science 
• Students learn content using real equipment used 
in most high-tech labs 
• Aligns with NGSS (Engineering and Technology) 
• This is the future of science!

8. BUSINESS 
B.  CONSIDERATION:  Data Storage and Backup Solution 
Pricing for a new Data Storage and Backup solution was solicited from 
EMC/Presidio, our current vendor, utilizing the Western States 
Contracting Alliance (WSCA) contract.  The configuration, pricing and 
contracting vehicle were reviewed by Kris Young, Dire ctor of 
Technology, John Foster, Convergent Technology Partners and Rick 
West, Assistant Superintendent for Business Services, and it is 
recommended the contract be awarded as stated below.  
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the contract for a new Data Storage and 
Backup solution be awarded to EMC/Presidio in the amount of 
$469,750.00, and that this amount be approved as an expenditure of the 
2014 Capital Projects Fund. 
 Yes: 
 No: 
167
Convergent Technology Partners  
801S. Saginaw  Suite 505, Flint, MI 48502  
810.720.3820  Fax 810.720.3886  
E-mail: info@ctpartners.net  
Website: www.ctpartners.net  
April 1, 2015 
Rick West 
Assistant Superintendent, Business Services 
Troy School District 
4400 Livernois 
Troy, Michigan 48098 
Mr. West: 
Over the past several months, we have been evaluating options for either purchasing a 
new maintenance contract on the existing data storage and backup systems or 
purchasing new state of the art solutions which will also provide five years of 
maintenance as part of the purchase price.  After reviewing the options, it is our 
recommendation to purchase new equipment with 5 years of extended warranty and 
maintenance.  This provides the District with a cost effective solution for state-of-the-
art products that are structured to accommodate 5% growth over each of the next 5 
years. If approved, we will purchase the equipment from our current providers, EMC 
Corporation and Presidio.  EMC is the manufacturer of the equipment and Presidio is the 
local implementation provider for EMC who will provide the on-site implementation 
services. 
The pricing for this project has been provided utilizing the Western States Contracting 
Alliance (WSCA) contract pricing which is already competitively bid.  This contract 
allows for a 27.5% discount off of list pricing and with additional negotiations, we were 
provided a total of 55% discount pricing off list for this purchase. Troy Schools is a 
member of WSCA and eligible to purchase off of this contract. 
The District has extensive experience with EMC and find their products to be a leader in 
the industry and their work to be of high quality.  Additionally, we verified several 
references for the product and in each case the entity spoke highly of the product and 
especially of the firm.  
We are recommending that the District accept the proposal from the EMC/Presidio team 
in the amount of $469,750.00.   
Please contact me if you have any questions about the bid process or this 
recommendation.

Sincerely, 
John C. Foster, RCDD

Technical HighlightsTechnical Highlights
177
• Leading “All Flash” storage array on the market
 No moveable parts so reliability is very high and its very fast
 (2) Storage Processors/Controllers 
 8Gb FC and 10Gb ISCSI Connectivity for Block
 20.00TB Raw Capacity 
 17.00TB Useable Capacity
 90 - 100TB of Logical Capacity
• Performance:
• Support of up to 150,000 IOPS
• Support of up to 800 VDI Users on a half populated storage node
• Support up to 2000 VDI users with a fully populated brick that only needs 2U of rack space
• Provide sub 1MS Latency on the VDI workloads
• Software:
• Built in Data De-Duplication and Compression to ensure that the array is at best utilization
• Easy to use management interface and software
• Performance Monitoring
• Grid based architecture
• Resources are globally shared (Easy scale)
• 99.9999% data availability

 B.  FIRST READING:   Revisions to Board Polic y B -1210 Closed 
Session, B -1301 Agenda Format for Regular Board of Education 
Meetings, and B-1401 Minutes  
 Members of the Board of Education have reviewed and proposed c hanges to 
update the following policies:   
  B-1201   Closed Session 
  B-1301   Agenda Format for Regular Board of Education Meetings 
  B-1401   Minutes  
 The first reading of these policies is presented at this evening’s meeting.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Board members will read changes to Board policies  
B-1210, B-1301, and B-1401 in a round-robin fashion.  
189
  MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B-1200 
B-1210 CLOSED SESSIONS 
The Board may hold a closed session at any time, as long as public 
notice is provided as set forth in Section B-1201. 
The Board may meet in closed session only after the purpose of the 
closed session has been announced and (except for closed sessions 
held for the purposes set forth in subsections 1, 2, and 3 below) five 
affirmative votes by roll call have been obtained.  The purpose and 
the roll call vote must be entered into the minutes of the meeting. 
The Board may not make any decisions requiring a vote at a 
meeting which is closed to the public.  The closed meetings are only 
for deliberation, review, or negotiation. 
The purposes for which a closed session may be called are limited 
to the following: 
 1. To consider the dismissal, suspension, or disciplining of or to 
hear complaints or charges brought against or to consider a 
periodic personal evaluation of  a public officer, employee, staff 
member or individual agent, if the named person requests a 
closed hearing. 
 2. To consider the dismissal, suspension, or disciplining of a 
student and when the student or his parent or guardian 
requests a closed hearing. 
 3. For strategy and negotiation sessions connected with the 
negotiation of a collective bargaining agreement. 
 4. To consider the purchase or lease of real property up to the time 
an option to purchase or lease that real property is obtained. 
 5. To consult with the attorney regarding trial or settlement 
strategy in connection with specific pending litigation, but this 
should occur only when an open meeting would have a 
detrimental financial effect upon the litigating or settlement 
positions of the Board. 
  MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B-1200 
190
B-1210 CLOSED SESSIONS (cont.)  
6. To review the specific contents of an application for 
employment or appointment to a public office, when the 
candidate requests that the application remain confidential.  
Interviews for employment or appointment to a public office 
will be held in an open meeting. 
7. To consider material exempt from discussion or disclosure by 
state or federal statute. 
Minutes of all closed sessions are recorded and include at least the 
following: 
1. The time, date, and place of the meeting. 
2. The members present and members absent. 
3.   Non-menbers present.  
34. The purpose or purposes for which the closed session is held.  
5.  Disciplinary hearing for a student or considering a complaint 
against an employee.   
These minutes are retained by the secretary of the Board or 
designee, are not available to the public, and are disclosed only if 
required by a civil action filed under Section 10, 11, or 13 of Act No. 
267, Public Acts 1976.  These minutes are destroyed one year and 
one day after approval of the minutes of the meeting at which the 
closed session was approved. 
Adopted: April 8, 1975

 D. PUBLIC COMMUNICATION (AGENDA ITEMS

 PUBLIC COMMUNICATION ( NON-AGENDAALL OTHER  
MINUTES     B-1400 
B-1401 MINUTES 
The minutes of Board meetings, except closed sessions, are 
recorded and printed for the secretary by the administrative staff 
and forwarded to members with the agenda for the next meeting 
for approval.  Corrected minutes shall show both the original entry 
and the correction. 
1. Minutes for all meetings include the following: 
 a. The time, date, and place of the meeting. 
 b. The members present and members absent. 
 c. A statement of the topics discussed and any decisions 
made at the meeting. 
 d. The purpose or purposes for which a closed session is held. 
 e.  All resolutions,  names of those who propose and second 
all resolutions, results of all votes taken including the 
number of those voting yes and no.  In the event of either a 
abstention, a roll call vote will be taken and the names of 
those voting yes and no will be recorded.  If a member 
abstains on a vote the reason for the abstention will be 
recorded.  The disposition of the vote will be noted.   
2. Proposed minutes must be available for public inspection at 
the address stated on the posted notice of the meeting within 
eight business days after a meeting.  Approved minutes must 
be available at the posted address within five business days 
after the meeting at which they are approved.

4.    The official minutes are bound and kept in the vault at the  
        administrative office.

10. OTHER 
 C. FIRST READING: Revisions to Board Policy J-2012 Anti-Bullying Policy 
 Members of the Board of Education and legal counsel have reviewed and 
proposed a minor change to the District’s Anti-Bullying Policy J-2012 in order to 
comply with Michigan Public Act 478 Revised School Code  pertaining to  
Cyberbullying.  
 The first reading of this policy is presented at this evening’s meeting.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  A Board member will read changes to Board policy J-2012 
Anti-Bullying.   
196
RIGHTS AND RESPONSIBILITIES, CODE OF STUDENT CONDUCT    J-2000  
J-2012 ANTI-BULLYING POLICY  
It is the policy of the District to provide a safe educational environment for all 
students and employees.  Bullying of a student or employee at school, in any 
building within the Troy School District boundaries or any TSD event is strictly 
prohibited. This policy shall be interpreted and enforced to protect all students 
and employees to equally prohibit bullying without regard to its subject matter 
or motivating animus.   
1.  Bullying.  Bullying of a student or employee is strictly prohibited.  For the 
purposes of this policy, "bullying" shall be defined as: 
Any written, verbal, or physical act, or any electronic communication, that is 
intended or that a reasonable person would know is likely to harm one or more 
students or employees either directly or indirectly by doing any of the 
following:   
a. Substantially interfering with educational opportunities, benefits, or 
programs of one or more students or employees;  
b. Adversely affecting a student or employee’s ability to participate in or 
benefit from the District's educational programs or activities by placing 
the student or employee in reasonable fear of physical harm or by 
causing substantial emotional distress;  
c. Having an actual and substantial detrimental effect on a student or 
employee's physical or mental health; or 
d. Causing substantial disruption in, or substantial interference with, the  
 orderly operation of the school.   
2.  Retaliation/False Accusation.   Retaliation or false accusation against a target of 
bullying, anyone reporting bullying, a witness, or another person with reliable 
information about an act of bullying is strictly prohibited.   
B. Reporting an Incident .  If a student, staff member, or other individual believes 
there has been an incident of bullying in violation of this policy, s/he shall promptly 
report such incident to the appropriate principal or designee,  
197
A. 
 Prohibited Conduct.  
 C. Investigation.   All reported allegations of a policy violation or related 
complaint about bullying shall be promptly and thoroughly investigated by the 
building principal or designee.  Each verified incident of bullying shall be 
documented and the resulting consequences including discipline and referrals will be 
reported annually to the school board. 
D. Notice to Parent/Guardian.  If the principal or designee determines that an 
incident of bullying has occurred, s/he shall promptly provide notification of same to 
the parent/guardian of the victim of the bullying and the parent/guardian of the 
perpetrator of the bullying.   
E. Annual Reports.  At least annually, the building principal or designee, or the 
Responsible School Official shall report all verified incidents of bullying and the 
resulting consequences, including any disciplinary action or referrals, to the Board of 
Education.   
F. Responsible School Official.   The superintendent shall be responsible for 
ensuring the proper implementation of this policy throughout the District.  The 
foregoing appointment shall not reduce or eliminate the duties and responsibilities of 
a principal or designee as described in this policy. 
G.  Posting/Publication of Policy.  Notice of this policy will be: (a) annually 
circulated to all students and staff, (b) posted in conspicuous locations in all school 
buildings and departments within the District, (c) annually discussed with students 
and employees, and (d) incorporated into the teacher, student, and parent/guardian 
handbooks. 
H.  Definitions  
1. "At school" means in a classroom, elsewhere on school premises, on a school 
bus or other school-related vehicle, or at a school-sponsored activity or event 
whether or not it is held on school premises.  "At school" also includes any 
conduct using a telecommunications access device  or telecommunications service 
provider  that occurs off school premises if the device or provider is owned by or 
under the District's control. 
2. "Telecommunications Access Device" means that term as defined in Section 
219a of the Michigan Penal Code, 1931 PA 328, MCL 750.219a, as may be 
amended from time to time.   
3. "Telecommunications Service Provider" means that term as defined in Section 
219a of the Michigan Penal Code, supra , as may be amended from time to time.   
REVISED :   M AY 6, 2014

   SCHOOL DISTRICT ELECTIONS     B‐0100 
B‐0102  BOARD MEMBERS’ TERM OF OFFICE 
The school electors shall elect members of the Board of Education 
for a term of six (6) years, commencing with the general school 
election in November of 2009 2016 and on the November general 
election date in the odd even years thereafter. 
Adopted:  January 15, 2008 
Revised:

   MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1202  PLACE OF MEETING 
Meetings of the Board are held at the District Administrative 
Centers, unless otherwise announced and posted unless 
otherwise announced at least twenty‐four hours in advance and 
notice posted in accordance with laws governing the District. 
Adopted:  April 8, 1975 
Revised:  March 15, 1977 
  January 16, 1979 
  April 15, 2003 
201
   MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1204  QUORUM 
Four  members  of  the  Board  constitute  a  quorum  for  the 
transaction of business.  If a member of the Board is not physically 
present at a duly held Board m eeting where a quorum is already 
present, the Board member who is  not physically present at the 
meeting may participate via remo te participation by means of 
telephone, teleconference, video co nference and/or other form of 
electronic means available to the Bo ard and that member as long as 
any attendee of the meeting can hear  and interact with Board member 
not in physical attendance. 
Absent Board member must have permission of presiding chair to 
participate remotely but may not vote. 
All meetings of the Board must comp ly with the Open Meetings Act, 
MCL 15.261 et seq, as that Act may be  amended from time to time.  
In the event of a conflict between this Policy and the Act, the Act 
shall control. 
Adopted:  January 16, 1979 
Revised:  July 10, 2012

203
MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1211  PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN BOARD OF EDUCATION 
MEETINGS 
The  Board  values  public  participation  and  recognizes  the  importance 
of  public  input  on  issues  affecting  the  Board  and  the  District.   This 
Policy  has  been  adopted  to  balance  the  importance  of  public 
participation  with  other  important  objectives,  including:  insuring  all 
community  members  a  fair  and  reasonable  opportunity  to  express 
their  views  to  the  Board;  completing  Board  business  effectively, 
efficiently and in  an  orderly manner; respecting other important  time 
commitments of Board members, District staff and audience members; 
and,  respecting  the  rights  of  Board  members,  District  staff,  students, 
and audience members. 
Public  participation  will  be  limited  to  60  minutes  per  Board  meeting.  
Generally,  Public  participation  at  the  beginning  of  a  meeting  will  be 
limited to agenda items that require action by the Board of Education 
at  the  current  meeting.   These  agenda  items  are  posted  as 
‘Consideration.’   All  other  agenda  items  and  any  non‐agenda  items 
will  be  addressed  during  public  participation  at  the  end  of  the 
meeting.there  will  be  30  minutes  of  public  participation  at  the 
beginning  of  the  meeting,  limited  to  agenda  items.   There  will  be  an 
additional 30 minutes of public participation at the end of the meeting, 
limited to non‐agenda items. 
Although the public participation portion of the meeting is limited, an 
exception may be made, if necessary, so that no one’s right to address 
the Board will be denied. 
Audience  members  who  wish  to  address  the  Board  must  complete  a 
Speaker Request Form.  Forms will be available 30 minutes prior to the 
start  of  the  meeting  and  must  be  completed  and  submitted  to  the 
Secretary  of  the  Board  of  Education  before  the  start  of  the  meeting.  
The  Board  President  or  presiding  officer  will  recognize  audience 
members for public participation subject to the following rules: 
 Each  person  wishing  to  speak  must  identify  self  by  name  and 
address. 
 Speakers will be taken in the order forms are collected. 
 Recognized speakers may be permitted to speak for up to three (3) 
minutes.  However, the President of the Board may reduce the time 
204
MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1211  PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN BOARD OF EDUCATION 
MEETINGS 
limit  for  each  speaker  in  order  to  accommodate  all  of  the 
individuals who wish to address the Board of Education. 
 Recognized  speakers  may  not  transfer  or  delegate  their  time  to 
others. 
 Delegations  who  wish  to  express  their  views  are  encouraged  to 
  select one speaker to present the delegation’s views to the   Board. 
 Each  member  of  a  delegation  wishing  to  speak  must  submit  a 
  separate Speaker Request form. 
 Persons who have completed Speaker Request Forms but have not 
had  an  opportunity  to  address  the  Board  or  to  complete  their 
presentations may be given the option of submitting their views in 
writing to the Secretary of the Board of Education, whose name and 
e‐mail  address  are  printed  on  each  Speaker  Request  Form.   The 
Secretary of the Board will forward all such written presentation to 
all Board members. 
 Speakers  shall  direct  their  comments  to  the  Board  and  not  to 
District staff or other audience members. 
 Speakers  should  not  expect  Board  members  to  answer  questions 
during  public  participation.   Questions  will  be  referred  to  the 
Superintendent  for  review  and  study  and,  if  appropriate,  future 
Board action. 
 Speakers  are  expected  to  express  themselves  responsibly  and 
courteously  and  with  due  respect  for  the  fact  their  views  and 
opinions  may  not  be  shared  by  all  present.   The  following  are 
examples of irresponsible or discourteous expression:  
 Denigrating  Board  members,  District  staff,  students,  or  other 
audience members. 
 Speculating  on  the  motives  of  Board  members,  District  staff,  
students, or other audience members. 
 Complaints  about  District  staff  or  students  that  have  not  been 
brought to the District’s attention through appropriate channels. 
205
MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1211  PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN BOARD OF EDUCATION 
MEETINGS 
 Speakers  may  not  express  complaints  against  Board  members  or 
District staff unrelated to their offices or jobs. 
 Speakers  may  not  express  complaints  against  Board  members  or 
District  staff  that  are  related  to  their  offices  or  job  unless  the 
affected  person  or  persons  have  been  notified  and  permitted  a 
reasonable opportunity to choose whether to hold a hearing on the 
complaint in open or closed session. 
 Audience members are expected to conduct themselves responsibly 
and  courteously  and  with  due  respect  for  the  fact  their  views  and 
opinions  may  not  be  shared  by  all  present.   Thus,  raucous  or 
disrespectful  expressions  of  agreement  or  disagreement  by 
audience members are not appropriate. 
 The  Board’s  President  or  presiding  officer  is  charged  with  the 
responsibility  for  insuring  that  public  participation  assists  the 
Board in discharging its responsibilities and is conducted according 
to  this  Policy.   Thus,  the  Board  President  or  presiding  officer  is 
authorized  to  enforce  this  Policy  by,  among  other  things: 
interrupting  presentations  to  remind  speakers  and  audience 
members of this Policy; dismissing previously recognized speakers 
who  violate  this  Policy;  requesting  speakers  or  audience  members 
to  leave  the  meeting  if  they  violate  the  Policy  and,  if  necessary, 
requesting  the  assistance  of  law  enforcement  officers  to  assist  in 
removing  speakers  or  audience  members  who  refuse  to  leave;  or, 
adjourning the meeting. 
The  Board  may  amend  or  waive  this  Policy,  or  specific  provisions 
thereof,  when,  in  the  Board’s  judgment,  amendment  or  waiver  is 
appropriate.
